1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
2: """
3: Code Commenter Pro - Documentation Generator.
4: 
5: Main generator script that creates documentation for source code files.
6: Supports multiple programming languages and generates professional file headers,
7: function docstrings, and inline comments.
8: 
9: Example:
10:     >>> generator = CommentGenerator("python")
11:     >>> header = generator.generate_file_header("example.py")
12:     >>> print(header)
13: 
14: Note:
15:     This module uses template-based generation for consistent documentation
16:     across different languages.
17: """
18: 
19: from pathlib import Path
20: from typing import Any, Dict, List
21: 
22: 
23: class CommentGenerator:
24:     """Generate comments and documentation for source code.
25: 
26:     This class provides methods to scan source code and generate appropriate
27:     documentation including file headers, function docstrings, and inline
28:     comments. It supports multiple programming languages through configurable
29:     templates.
30: 
31:     Attributes:
32:         language: Target programming language (e.g., 'python', 'javascript').
33:         templates: Dictionary of documentation templates for the target language.
34: 
35:     Example:
36:         >>> generator = CommentGenerator(language="python")
37:         >>> header = generator.generate_file_header("my_module.py")
38:         >>> print(header)
39:     """
40:     def __init__(self, language: str = "python"):
41:         """Initialize the comment generator.
42: 
43:         Args:
44:             language: Target programming language. Defaults to "python".
45:                 Supported languages include "python", "javascript", "java", etc.
46: 
47:         Example:
48:             >>> gen = CommentGenerator("javascript")
49:             >>> gen.language
50:             'javascript'
51:         """
52:         self.language = language
53:         self.templates = self._load_templates()
54: 
55:     def generate_file_header(self, filepath: str) -> str:
56:         """Generate a file header comment.
57: 
58:         Creates a standard file header with the filename and generator attribution.
59:         The header uses a triple-quoted string format suitable for the target language.
60: 
61:         Args:
62:             filepath: Path to the source file. Can be absolute or relative.
63: 
64:         Returns:
65:             str: A formatted file header comment as a string.
66: 
67:         Example:
68:             >>> gen = CommentGenerator("python")
69:             >>> header = gen.generate_file_header("/src/utils.py")
70:             >>> print(header)
71:         """
72:         filename = Path(filepath).name
73:         return f'"""\n{filename}\n\nGenerated by Code Commenter Pro.\n"""'
74: 
75:     def generate_function_doc(self, func_name: str, params: List[str], returns: str) -> str:
76:         """Generate function documentation.
77: 
78:         Creates a Google-style docstring for a function with parameter and
79:         return value descriptions.
80: 
81:         Args:
82:             func_name: Name of the function being documented.
83:             params: List of parameter names for the function.
84:             returns: Description of the function's return value.
85: 
86:         Returns:
87:             str: A formatted docstring with Args and Returns sections.
88: 
89:         Example:
90:             >>> gen = CommentGenerator("python")
91:             >>> doc = gen.generate_function_doc("my_func", ["x", "y"], "int")
92:             >>> print(doc)
93:         """
94:         doc = f'"""\n{func_name}\n\n'
95: 
96:         for param in params:
97:             doc += f"Args:\n    {param}: Description\n\n"
98: 
99:         doc += f"Returns:\n    {returns}\n"
100:         doc += '"""'
101: 
102:         return doc
103: 
104:     def scan_and_document(self, source_code: str) -> str:
105:         """Return the source code unchanged.
106: 
107:         This method is reserved for future documentation generation workflows
108:         that analyze and transform source code.
109: 
110:         Args:
111:             source_code: The raw source code string.
112: 
113:         Returns:
114:             str: The provided source code.
115:         """
116:         return source_code
117: 
118:     def _load_templates(self) -> Dict[str, Any]:
119:         """Load documentation templates for the target language.
120: 
121:         Loads template dictionaries used for generating consistent
122:         documentation across different code elements.
123: 
124:         Returns:
125:             dict: A dictionary of templates keyed by documentation type.
126:         """
127:         return {}

132:     print("Code Commenter Pro initialized.")

1: """
2: Code Commenter - Core Implementation.
3: 
4: Automatic code documentation and comment generation tool that supports
5: multiple programming languages including Python, JavaScript, and Java.
6: 
7: This module provides the main CodeCommenter class for generating docstrings,
8: inline comments, and API documentation from source code.
9: 
10: Example:
11:     >>> from scripts.code_commenter import generate_docs
12:     >>> result = generate_docs("path/to/file.py")
13:     >>> print(result)
14: """
15: 
16: import os
17: import re
18: from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional
19: 
20: 
21: class CodeCommenter:
22:     """Automatically generate professional documentation for code.
23: 
24:     This class provides methods to analyze source code and generate appropriate
25:     documentation including docstrings, inline comments, and API documentation.
26:     Supports multiple programming languages through configurable templates.
27: 
28:     Attributes:
29:         language: The target programming language (e.g., 'python', 'javascript').
30:         template: Dictionary containing language-specific formatting rules.
31: 
32:     Example:
33:         >>> commenter = CodeCommenter("python")
34:         >>> docstring = commenter.generate_docstring("my_function", ["x", "y"], "int")
35:     """
36: 
37:     # Language-specific templates for documentation formatting
38:     LANGUAGE_TEMPLATES = {
39:         "python": {
40:             "docstring_start": '"""',
41:             "docstring_end": '"""',
42:             "comment_prefix": "#",
43:             "inline_format": "# {comment}"
44:         },
45:         "javascript": {
46:             "docstring_start": "/**",
47:             "docstring_end": " */",
48:             "comment_prefix": "//",
49:             "inline_format": "// {comment}"
50:         },
51:         "java": {
52:             "docstring_start": "/**",
53:             "docstring_end": " */",
54:             "comment_prefix": "//",
55:             "inline_format": "// {comment}"
56:         }
57:     }
58: 
59:     def __init__(self, language: str = "python"):
60:         """Initialize the CodeCommenter with a target language.
61: 
62:         Args:
63:             language: The programming language for documentation generation.
64:                 Defaults to "python". Supported values: "python", "javascript", "java".
65: 
66:         Example:
67:             >>> commenter = CodeCommenter("javascript")
68:             >>> commenter.language
69:             'javascript'
70:         """
71:         self.language = language
72:         self.template = self.LANGUAGE_TEMPLATES.get(language, self.LANGUAGE_TEMPLATES["python"])
73: 
74:     def generate_docstring(self, function_name: str, params: List[str], returns: Optional[str] = None) -> str:
75:         """Build a language-appropriate docstring for a function.
76: 
77:         Combines the function name, parameter descriptions, and return value
78:         documentation into a formatted docstring using the configured template.
79: 
80:         Args:
81:             function_name: Name of the function being documented.
82:             params: List of parameter names for the function.
83:             returns: Optional description of the return value.
84: 
85:         Returns:
86:             str: A formatted docstring with Args and Returns sections.
87: 
88:         Example:
89:             >>> commenter = CodeCommenter("python")
90:             >>> doc = commenter.generate_docstring("add", ["a", "b"], "int")
91:         """
92:         docstring = f"{self.template['docstring_start']}\n"
93:         docstring += f"{function_name} - Function documentation.\n\n"
94: 
95:         if params:
96:             docstring += "Args:\n"
97:             for param in params:
98:                 docstring += f"    {param}: Description for {param}\n"
99: 
100:         if returns:
101:             docstring += f"\nReturns:\n    {returns}: Description\n"
102: 
103:         docstring += f"{self.template['docstring_end']}"
104:         return docstring
105: 
106:     def add_inline_comments(self, code: str, comments: Dict[int, str]) -> str:
107:         """Append inline comments to the end of specified source lines.
108: 
109:         Uses the language-specific inline comment format to add each comment
110:         after the corresponding line in the provided code string.
111: 
112:         Args:
113:             code: The source code string to be commented.
114:             comments: Dictionary mapping line numbers (0-indexed) to comment text.
115: 
116:         Returns:
117:             str: Source code with inline comments added at specified lines.
118: 
119:         Example:
120:             >>> commenter = CodeCommenter("python")
121:             >>> code = "x = 1\\ny = 2"
122:             >>> commented = commenter.add_inline_comments(code, {0: "Initialize x"})
123:             >>> print(commented)
124:         """
125:         lines = code.split('\n')
126:         for line_num, comment in comments.items():
127:             if 0 <= line_num < len(lines):
128:                 comment_line = self.template['inline_format'].format(comment=comment)
129:                 lines[line_num] = f"{lines[line_num]}  {comment_line}"
130:         return '\n'.join(lines)
131: 
132:     def process_file(self, file_path: str) -> Dict[str, Any]:
133:         """Read a source file and return metadata about it.
134: 
135:         Detects the programming language from the file extension and returns
136:         a summary including the file path, detected language, and line count.
137: 
138:         Args:
139:             file_path: Path to the source code file to process.
140: 
141:         Returns:
142:             dict: A dictionary containing:
143:                 - status: "processed" on success, "error" on failure
144:                 - file: The file path that was processed
145:                 - language: The detected programming language
146:                 - lines: Number of lines in the file
147:                 - message: Error message if status is "error"
148: 
149:         Example:
150:             >>> commenter = CodeCommenter()
151:             >>> result = commenter.process_file("src/main.py")
152:             >>> result['language']
153:             'python'
154:         """
155:         if not os.path.exists(file_path):
156:             return {"status": "error", "message": "File not found"}
157: 
158:         with open(file_path, 'r') as f:
159:             content = f.read()
160: 
161:         ext = os.path.splitext(file_path)[1]
162:         language_map = {'.py': 'python', '.js': 'javascript', '.java': 'java'}
163:         self.language = language_map.get(ext, 'python')
164:         self.template = self.LANGUAGE_TEMPLATES[self.language]
165: 
166:         return {
167:             "status": "processed",
168:             "file": file_path,
169:             "language": self.language,
170:             "lines": len(content.split('\n'))
171:         }
172: 
173:     def generate_api_docs(self, code: str) -> str:
174:         """Extract function definitions and produce Markdown API docs.
175: 
176:         Parses the provided source code to identify function signatures and
177:         emits a Markdown summary of each function and its parameters.
178: 
179:         Args:
180:             code: The source code string to analyze.
181: 
182:         Returns:
183:             str: Markdown-formatted API documentation with function signatures
184:                 and parameter information.
185: 
186:         Example:
187:             >>> commenter = CodeCommenter("python")
188:             >>> code = "def hello(name): return f'Hello {name}'"
189:             >>> docs = commenter.generate_api_docs(code)
190:         """
191:         docs = "# API Documentation\n\n"
192: 
193:         if self.language == "python":
194:             functions = re.findall(r'def\s+(\w+)\s*\(([^)]*)\)', code)
195:             for func_name, params in functions:
196:                 docs += f"## `{func_name}()`\n\n"
197:                 docs += f"**Parameters:** {params if params else 'None'}\n\n"
198:                 docs += "**Returns:** Documented return value\n\n"
199: 
200:         return docs
201: 
202: 
203: def comment_code(code: str, language: str = "python") -> str:
204:     """Return a basic docstring stub for the supplied code snippet.
205: 
206:     Convenience function that creates a CodeCommenter instance and generates
207:     a formatted docstring without requiring an explicit class instance.
208: 
209:     Args:
210:         code: The source code string to be commented.
211:         language: Target programming language. Defaults to "python".
212: 
213:     Returns:
214:         str: A generated docstring for the code.
215: 
216:     Example:
217:         >>> commented = comment_code("def hello(): pass", "python")
218:     """
219:     commenter = CodeCommenter(language)
220:     return commenter.generate_docstring("function", [])
221: 
222: 
223: def generate_docs(file_path: str) -> Dict[str, Any]:
224:     """Process a source file and return its documentation metadata.
225: 
226:     Main entry point for the code commenter module. Reads the file, detects
227:     its language from the extension, and returns a summary of the result.
228: 
229:     Args:
230:         file_path: Path to the source code file to document.
231: 
232:     Returns:
233:         dict: A dictionary containing processing results with keys:
234:             - status: "processed" or "error"
235:             - file: The file path
236:             - language: Detected programming language
237:             - lines: Number of lines in the file
238:             - message: Error message if applicable
239: 
240:     Example:
241:         >>> result = generate_docs("src/utils.py")
242:         >>> print(result['language'])
243:     """
244:     commenter = CodeCommenter()
245:     return commenter.process_file(file_path)

(End of file - total 245 lines)

1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
2: """
3: Code Commenter Pro - Python Template Library.
4: 
5: This module provides comment and docstring templates for Python code
6: documentation. It includes generators for creating standardized docstrings
7: and inline comment templates for common documentation patterns.
8: 
9: Example:
10:     >>> from templates.python_template import DocstringGenerator
11:     >>> gen = DocstringGenerator()

106:         str: The code line with the inline comment appended.

35:     """
36: 
37:     # Language-specific templates for documentation formatting
38:     LANGUAGE_TEMPLATES = {
39:         "python": {
40:             "docstring_start": '"""',
41:             "docstring_end": '"""',
42:             "comment_prefix": "#",
43:             "inline_format": "# {comment}"
44:         },
45:         "javascript": {
46:             "docstring_start": "/**",
47:             "docstring_end": " */",
48:             "comment_prefix": "//",
49:             "inline_format": "// {comment}"
50:         },
51:         "java": {
52:             "docstring_start": "/**",
53:             "docstring_end": " */",
54:             "comment_prefix": "//",
55:             "inline_format": "// {comment}"
56:         }
57:     }
58: 
59:     def __init__(self, language: str = "python"):
60:         """Initialize the CodeCommenter with a target language.
61: 
62:         Args:
63:             language: The programming language for documentation generation.
64:                 Defaults to "python". Supported values: "python", "javascript", "java".
65: 
66:         Example:
67:             >>> commenter = CodeCommenter("javascript")
68:             >>> commenter.language
69:             'javascript'
70:         """
71:         self.language = language
72:         self.template = self.LANGUAGE_TEMPLATES.get(language, self.LANGUAGE_TEMPLATES["python"])
73: 
74:     def generate_docstring(self, function_name: str, params: List[str], returns: Optional[str] = None) -> str:
75:         """Build a language-appropriate docstring for a function.
76: 
77:         Combines the function name, parameter descriptions, and return value
78:         documentation into a formatted docstring using the configured template.
79: 
80:         Args:
81:             function_name: Name of the function being documented.
82:             params: List of parameter names for the function.
83:             returns: Optional description of the return value.
84: 
85:         Returns:
86:             str: A formatted docstring with Args and Returns sections.
87: 
88:         Example:
89:             >>> commenter = CodeCommenter("python")
90:             >>> doc = commenter.generate_docstring("add", ["a", "b"], "int")
91:         """
92:         docstring = f"{self.template['docstring_start']}\n"
93:         docstring += f"{function_name} - Function documentation.\n\n"
94: 
95:         if params:
96:             docstring += "Args:\n"
97:             for param in params:
98:                 docstring += f"    {param}: Description for {param}\n"
99: 
100:         if returns:
101:             docstring += f"\nReturns:\n    {returns}: Description\n"
102: 
103:         docstring += f"{self.template['docstring_end']}"
104:         return docstring
105: 
106:     def add_inline_comments(self, code: str, comments: Dict[int, str]) -> str:
107:         """Append inline comments to the end of specified source lines.
108: 
109:         Uses the language-specific inline comment format to add each comment
110:         after the corresponding line in the provided code string.
111: 
112:         Args:
113:             code: The source code string to be commented.
114:             comments: Dictionary mapping line numbers (0-indexed) to comment text.
115: 
116:         Returns:
117:             str: Source code with inline comments added at specified lines.
118: 
119:         Example:
120:             >>> commenter = CodeCommenter("python")
121:             >>> code = "x = 1\\ny = 2"
122:             >>> commented = commenter.add_inline_comments(code, {0: "Initialize x"})
123:             >>> print(commented)
124:         """
125:         lines = code.split('\n')
126:         for line_num, comment in comments.items():
127:             if 0 <= line_num < len(lines):
128:                 comment_line = self.template['inline_format'].format(comment=comment)
129:                 lines[line_num] = f"{lines[line_num]}  {comment_line}"
130:         return '\n'.join(lines)
131: 
132:     def process_file(self, file_path: str) -> Dict[str, Any]:
133:         """Read a source file and return metadata about it.
134: 
135:         Detects the programming language from the file extension and returns
136:         a summary including the file path, detected language, and line count.
137: 
138:         Args:
139:             file_path: Path to the source code file to process.
140: 
141:         Returns:
142:             dict: A dictionary containing:
143:                 - status: "processed" on success, "error" on failure
144:                 - file: The file path that was processed
145:                 - language: The detected programming language
146:                 - lines: Number of lines in the file
147:                 - message: Error message if status is "error"
148: 
149:         Example:
150:             >>> commenter = CodeCommenter()
151:             >>> result = commenter.process_file("src/main.py")
152:             >>> result['language']
153:             'python'
154:         """
155:         if not os.path.exists(file_path):
156:             return {"status": "error", "message": "File not found"}
157: 
158:         with open(file_path, 'r') as f:
159:             content = f.read()
160: 
161:         ext = os.path.splitext(file_path)[1]
162:         language_map = {'.py': 'python', '.js': 'javascript', '.java': 'java'}
163:         self.language = language_map.get(ext, 'python')
164:         self.template = self.LANGUAGE_TEMPLATES[self.language]
165: 
166:         return {
167:             "status": "processed",
168:             "file": file_path,
169:             "language": self.language,
170:             "lines": len(content.split('\n'))
171:         }
172: 
173:     def generate_api_docs(self, code: str) -> str:
174:         """Extract function definitions and produce Markdown API docs.
175: 
176:         Parses the provided source code to identify function signatures and
177:         emits a Markdown summary of each function and its parameters.
178: 
179:         Args:
180:             code: The source code string to analyze.
181: 
182:         Returns:
183:             str: Markdown-formatted API documentation with function signatures
184:                 and parameter information.
185: 
186:         Example:
187:             >>> commenter = CodeCommenter("python")
188:             >>> code = "def hello(name): return f'Hello {name}'"
189:             >>> docs = commenter.generate_api_docs(code)
190:         """
191:         docs = "# API Documentation\n\n"
192: 
193:         if self.language == "python":
194:             functions = re.findall(r'def\s+(\w+)\s*\(([^)]*)\)', code)
195:             for func_name, params in functions:
196:                 docs += f"## `{func_name}()`\n\n"
197:                 docs += f"**Parameters:** {params if params else 'None'}\n\n"
198:                 docs += "**Returns:** Documented return value\n\n"
199: 
200:         return docs
201: 
202: 
203: def comment_code(code: str, language: str = "python") -> str:
204:     """Return a basic docstring stub for the supplied code snippet.
205: 
206:     Convenience function that creates a CodeCommenter instance and generates
207:     a formatted docstring without requiring an explicit class instance.
208: 
209:     Args:
210:         code: The source code string to be commented.
211:         language: Target programming language. Defaults to "python".
212: 
213:     Returns:
214:         str: A generated docstring for the code.
215: 
216:     Example:
217:         >>> commented = comment_code("def hello(): pass", "python")
218:     """
219:     commenter = CodeCommenter(language)
220:     return commenter.generate_docstring("function", [])
221: 
222: 
223: def generate_docs(file_path: str) -> Dict[str, Any]:

227:     its language from the extension, and returns a summary of the result.

241:         >>> result = generate_docs("src/utils.py")

244:     commenter = CodeCommenter()
